three (V3-O07, spec section 28.4; review 3 section 7 and the R3 instructions), recorded verbatim from real runs.

  (unchanged since r2 except future_sizes.rs, which is new in r3)

Probe method (unchanged, head_cost.rs; as in r2): every category runs 1000 untimed warm-up operations (execution:
50), then one counted batch of 2000 operations (execution: 200) under allocation-counter (current thread only),
then 9 timed batches (execution: 5). "allocations/op" is the allocation COUNT per operation (realloc counts) and
"bytes/op" the allocated BYTES per operation (allocation traffic, not peak resident or retained memory).
"median/min ns/op" are over the timed batches; the counting allocator is active during them in every tree. The
execution row uses the probe's TCP_NODELAY loopback responder and includes building the request and its boxed
IntoFuture.

Protocol:
  Block A (trees v2.1, 4c7b894, 1477d3a; worktree at 1477d3a, clean): 4 blocks x 9 rounds, one run per tree per
    round, the order of the three trees rotated cyclically every round (each tree takes each position equally
    often): 36 runs per tree, 108 processes, every one exited 0. Blocks 1-2 ran first; blocks 3-4 were added
    right after (same executables, same script) to tighten the execution comparison, whose per-block deltas
    varied; all four blocks are reported, nothing was discarded.
  Block B (trees v2.1, 4c7b894, 1477d3a, 2b82432; worktree at 2b82432, clean): 4 blocks x 8 rounds, the order of
    the four trees rotated cyclically every round: 32 runs per tree, 128 processes, every one exited 0.
  Commands (exit code after every run in the logs):
    <scratch>/r3/bin/head_cost_<tree>            (tree = v21 | v3r = 4c7b894 | v3c = 1477d3a | v3n = 2b82432)
  Analysis: a scratch script parses the logs, asserts that counts and bytes are identical in every run of a tree,
  and computes the median of the per-run medians and the minimum over all timed batches.

Supporting instruction counts (not timing): valgrind 3.22 `--tool=callgrind` over one whole head_cost run per
tree (same executables; each plan category runs 21,001 operations: 1 printed plan, 1000 warm-up, 2000 counted,
18000 timed). Inclusive instructions of the per-category entry points are quoted in the derived section.

Reading the numbers (attribution: ATTRIBUTION.md, "r3 remeasurement"):

- Counts and bytes (deterministic; identical in every run of a tree). 1477d3a equals 4c7b894 in every row
  except managed execution: 63 = 63 = 63 allocations; 24,647 (v2.1) -> 27,991 (4c7b894) -> 26,215 B/op
  (1477d3a): the layout correction removes 1,776 B/op, leaving +1,568 B/op (+6.4%) versus v2.1. Construction
  +8 B, the three plain plan rows -24 B and the rate plan +330 B versus v2.1 are unchanged since r2.
- Construction and binding of 1477d3a and 2b82432 stay faster than v2.1 in every block (direct -9% to -11%,
  unbound -4% to -7%, binding -15% to -17%); 4c7b894 likewise except one slow block (B4: direct +27%, unbound
  +20% median, with minima still below v2.1).
- Planning, 1477d3a versus v2.1 (block A, all 36 runs): show +6.2%, search +4.1%, protected +7.1%, limited
  +0.9%; block B reproduces this (+7.2%, +4.6%, +8.4%, +0.7%). 4c7b894 versus v2.1: +3.8%, +2.6%, +4.8%,
  -0.4% (block B: +6.0%, +2.6%, +5.9%, -0.6%).
- 1477d3a versus 4c7b894 on the planning rows is +1.1% to +2.4% over all runs of both blocks (per block -1.5% to +4.0%)
  although no planning source differs between them (the only crates/ change is execute/mod.rs) and callgrind
  counts 0.0-0.2% FEWER instructions per plan for 1477d3a. That is a same-source, same-host,
  interleaved measurement of about +2% from code placement alone (the execute/mod.rs change moves code in the
  same crate). It supports keeping about +/-5% as the resolution of the time comparison, and it shows that
  part of the +4..+7% planning residual versus v2.1 can be layout rather than work.
- Managed execution, 1477d3a versus 4c7b894: +0.1% (block A) and +3.7% (block B) in median time; versus v2.1
  +8.1% (A) and +2.9% (B). The -1,776 B/op and -1.7% instructions per execution are therefore not visible as
  a time change within this probe's resolution; execution times are bimodal on this host in every tree (fast
  batches about 31-40 us, slow ones about 60-110 us, see the per-run medians), so the minimum columns compare
  modes, not code.
- After 2b82432 (block B): planning allocations fall by 3 (show, protected, limited) and 7 (search) per plan
  (48 -> 45, 81 -> 74, 40 -> 37, 56 -> 53) and bytes by 32-64 B; plan time falls 9-14% versus 1477d3a in every
  block (show -12.2%, search -11.6%, protected -13.6%, limited -9.1% over all runs), matching the -12.5% /
  -13.1% instructions of the show and search plans. Versus v2.1 the plan rows are now -5.9%, -7.5%, -6.3%,
  -8.5%. CAUTION: this comes from making a classifier that v2.1 has in identical form cheaper, not from
  removing the v3-specific planning work; the like-for-like planning residual versus v2.1 is the 1477d3a
  comparison above (partly layout, see ATTRIBUTION.md). Construction, binding and execution are unchanged by
  2b82432 (execution -0.5% time, identical counts and bytes).

Record hygiene (R3): trailing spaces at line ends of verbatim tool output (callgrind annotations and
similar) and trailing blank lines were stripped so the file passes `git diff --check`; no other byte changed.
